# Lab Timesheet — current work

What is being worked on now, what comes next, and what is waiting on someone.
This file tracks progress only. What the system must do is in
[`.sdd/specs/`](.sdd/specs); how a feature will be built goes in that feature's
`PLAN.md`, and its task breakdown in `TASKS.md`. The rules for working here are
in [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) and [`.sdd/constitution.md`](.sdd/constitution.md).

**Last updated:** 2026-09-30 · **Branch:** `work/integration/attendance`.
Step 6 implementation branch created on 22 September 2026. Task A-01 established the baseline.
Documentation work from checkpoint `c443670` through D34–D39 was completed on `work/fix/docs/module-feature-specs`
at commit `2945975fa43d1f8eb4ddd8b2f90b9c02e800b39ed0cd8f73d1a4aaf2dc90a9db`. Step 6 branches directly from that
commit ahead of PR #14 merge per maintainer approval. PR #14 merged into `main` on 22 September 2026 as `7a0d46f`,
whose tree equals `2945975`.
Backup checkpoint `c443670` is preserved on Gitea under branch `work/fix/docs/backup-before-feature-split-20260920`
and tag `backup/pre-feature-split-20260920`.

## Where the project is

| Stage | State |
|---|---|
| Delivery, Iterations 1–4 | Built. Every tracked item is done; the Iteration 3 and 4 integration gates were never run. |
| Specification | Eight module contracts and 31 feature SPECs; [entry map](.sdd/specs/README.md). D34 organizes workflows; D35 settles Task creation/unblocking, cancelled-Project internship readiness and current-session logout. D36 additionally excludes soft-deleted Tasks from internship readiness. D37 settles assignee non-deleted Task transitions, aligns DB-011 and DB-012 status constraints with V1__baseline.sql, and defines complete invitation and exit-request state transition tables. D38 settles the account state machine, sign-in by account state, the email delivery states and password-reset eligibility, and removes the parallel `feature-*` document tree. D39 records the status predicates the migration must change beyond the status constraints. Both were corrected on two rounds of independent review. D40 puts every `PLAN.md` and `TASKS.md` beside its feature SPEC, with no exception, and adds the platform features Architecture, Authorization, Security and Data model, whose rows moved unchanged. Current catalogue: 311 rules and 167 scenarios. §22.3 is empty, so §22.2's condition on it is met; acceptance by the authority in §1.1 is still required. |
| Constitution | Locked, version `2.0.6`. |
| Technical plans (`PLAN.md`) | Under `D40` each plan sits beside its feature SPEC. Parts B and C now live in the [Authorization](.sdd/specs/platform/features/authorization/PLAN.md) and [Data model](.sdd/specs/platform/features/data-model/PLAN.md) plans, and two tasks of part B became parts D and E of the [Architecture](.sdd/specs/platform/features/architecture/PLAN.md) and [Security](.sdd/specs/platform/features/security/PLAN.md) plans; part A has moved to the [Architecture plan](.sdd/specs/platform/features/architecture/PLAN.md) after `A-12`. Part A, the module boundaries of `ARC-005` and `ARC-006`, was approved on 22 September 2026 with its tasks A-01 to A-12 in the [Architecture tasks](.sdd/specs/platform/features/architecture/TASKS.md), after three review rounds that day; step 6 built and committed all twelve tasks. A-13 was approved and completed on 24 September 2026 after the step-7 comparison. The quota of `ATT-003` waits on the stored policy versions (C-01); the notices of `NOT-011` keep `SYSTEM` unless a feature plan adds codes. Feature plans belong beside their feature SPECs and inherit MODULE.md; no empty plans were created. |

Parts B and C were approved on 25 September 2026 with their plans. B-01 was completed on 26 September 2026 on `work/fix/platform/authorization-matrix` and awaits merge: its matrix test covers all 144 cells, with 8 open cells for B-02 and B-03 and 10 operations the code does not yet offer. D-01 and E-01 were revised on 24 September 2026: D-01 rewrites the native deletion as JPQL under `D42` and bans the direct-SQL entry points listed in D.2 of the plan across the production sources, E-01 reads headers and cookies from a real server and sees every case fail first, and both close their constitution gaps under Amendment; parts D and E were approved on 24 September 2026. D-01 merged into `main` as `8587d68`; E-01 merged into `main` as `0c98651` on 25 September 2026. |

- `D38` closes the last entries of §22.3. `ACC-014` names every account transition, `ACC-028` deactivates an account created in error while still pending, `ACC-029` reinstates a deactivated account to the state its record allows, keeping a lock it had, and `ACC-030` refuses sign-in to every account that is not `ACTIVE`. `DB-022` loosens V1 by exactly the two rows those rules need and keeps its non-blank hash; by trigger, the activation timestamp is set only by activation and the lock timestamp only by locking and cleared only by unlocking, so the schema itself forces the three outcomes of `ACC-029`. `NOT-012` states the five email delivery states and their transitions. `SEC-015` states password-reset eligibility, keeps an Admin's lock through a reset, and clears the throttle. Eight scenarios cover them. `ACC-028`, `ACC-029`, `ACC-030`, `DB-022` and the throttle clause need a migration or new code; no Java, schema or dependency changed here.
- Independent review of `b008c1f` and `399dc17` found two false claims (that `DB-022` tightened the schema, and that `D39` was a single omission), three partly true, and three further errors: a rollback that would have duplicated every rule, a wrong rule cited for activation, and two scenario counts. Every finding was confirmed against the repository before `D38` and `D39` were corrected in place, each with a record of what was wrong. The correction also found the spec map's tree, `CLAUDE.md` and this file still describing the removed folders, and a fourth member of the class in `ck_attendance_corrections_decided_at`.
- A second review of `b36842d` found the lock still removable by one update that moves a locked `DEACTIVATED` account to `ACTIVE`, the throttle clearing labelled `ENTERPRISE-BACKED` on an on-premises source, and a seventh unrecorded predicate. It also rejected a proposal to make a Project's activation timestamp write-once, which rested on a claim that reports read it; nothing does. Each finding was confirmed before `D38` and `D39` were corrected again: the lock is set only by locking and cleared only by unlocking, the label is `ADAPTED` on the smart lockout page, and the criterion for protecting a column is that a rule reads it to choose a later transition.
- `D39` records a criterion and a starting list, not a complete count. A predicate is a member when, once the rules apply, it refuses a lawful row or admits an unlawful one, and a history-bound member when tightening it would also refuse stored rows that were lawful when written. The criterion applies to every rule and every table; the full audit is plan work. On the eight tables examined, those the pending decisions `D12`, `D32` and `D38` change, 19 of their 54 check, exclusion and unique predicates are members, two of them history-bound; outside them, `ck_notifications_email_payload` and `ck_attendance_policy_versions_quota` have been found so far. `DB-018` requires a withdrawal time and names the approval a cancelled request carries; `DB-019` accepts a cancelled Project with or without an activation timestamp; `AC-DB-010`–`AC-DB-012` cover them. Stored `CANCELLED` rows with no decision time become `WITHDRAWN` once the database's migration history is identified.
- `D38` Part 1b defines the `Status` field in the specification map, which nothing had defined and no check had read. An audit found no document still naming an open question, so twenty-five move to `APPROVED BUSINESS BASELINE`, each with its changelog entry, and the sentences pointing at closed gaps were corrected. A new check binds the field to the open questions and was proved in both directions.
- `D38` Part 1 removes the parallel `feature-*` document tree. Its changelogs are kept in the owning module's changelog under *Retained history*: the ninety-one entries committed at `c443670`, all present, eighty-two byte for byte and nine with a link retargeted, plus eight `D34` entries that were never committed on their own. `feature-platform/PLAN.md` moved to `.sdd/specs/platform/PLAN.md`, successor links were retargeted, and the sixteen files were deleted.

- **Recorded only.** `LayerStructureTest`, `ModuleBoundaryCycleTest` and `AttendanceAndTaskWorkSeparationTest` check the placement of production code under `src/main/java/com/lab/labtimesheet` only, so a production class outside that directory is caught by none of them. A-13 does not change this.
- **Flyway-only check.** Step 7 did not establish the Flyway-only clause of `AC-ARC-001`. `PlatformFoundationTest#flywayCreatesApprovedPostgresCatalog` proves the migrated PostgreSQL catalog has the expected shape after application startup, but does not assert that Flyway is the only schema authority. `FailedMigrationReadinessIntegrationTest` proves a failed Flyway migration stops its isolated context and rolls its DDL back; it does not exercise JPA or exclude another schema-creation path. The repository config sets Hibernate `ddl-auto` to `validate`, but neither test structurally guards the full Flyway-only claim.

## Next

The specification is frozen for planning. `D38` and `D39` were corrected over three review rounds and are now
frozen; they change only if the migration fails or the business asks for a change. The maintainer decided
on 22 September 2026 that `D34`–`D37` get no separate document review: `D34` is organization that `D38`
Part 1 has since passed through, and the rules of `D35`–`D37` are checked when each plan traces the rules it
implements. Next is the plan phase: feature plans for settled workflows with module dependency checks, then
tasks, code and validation, including the module boundaries of `D28`. Savepoint before it:
`savepoint/pre-module-split-2026-09-17`.

Numbered as the steps of `D28`, so that "step 6" means the same thing here, in `ADR-006`

## Evidence for Task A-02 (Cycle test, 22 September 2026)

- **Scope:** A-02 remains uncommitted on `work/fix/architecture/module-boundaries`; `HEAD` remains `30967a7`, the reviewed A-01 correction. Only the architecture test and its boundary-list resource are changed.
- **Placement list:** `src/test/resources/architecture/module-boundaries.tsv` contains only temporary `P` rows: the non-module packages and classes whose target module differs from the package-derived module. Module packages, `platform`, `config` and the root package are derived by the test; `account`, `integration` and `task` are mapped only by their `P` rows, so a class left behind when its row is deleted fails the test, which was seen once by deleting the `task` row. Exact rows override package rows; stale rows, duplicate matches and unmapped production classes fail the test.
- **Allowance list:** The same resource contains `A` rows with individual source and target simple names and a resolution label. The list is historical and is permitted to shrink only. A stale allowance, a new reference, a config reference, an ambiguous name, or a cycle fails the test. The two false matches from the previous attempt (`NotificationService -> Task` from ordinary string text and `InternshipLifecycleGuard -> Task` from Javadoc) are excluded; only JPQL-like strings naming JPA entities are scanned.
- **Analyzer comparison:** `node scripts/module-boundaries.cjs` reports 692 edges before resolution, the single baseline strongly connected group, and zero cycles after its R1/R3/R4/R7/R8/R9/R6 resolutions. The test's 31 individual allowance references are all code references inside the module pairs the analyzer resolves by R1, R3, R4, R7 and R8; no analyzer-only or stale item was added. Counts: R1 4, R3 10, R4 1 (`SmtpConfigurationService` to `AccountService`), R7 13, R8 3 (the three calendar screens calling `currentBusinessDate`), R10 0, since `SecurityProperties` is placed in `platform`.
- **Failure evidence:** Five focused tests pass: the real graph check plus synthetic failures for an artificial cycle, a config reference, a new reference between already-connected modules, and an ARC-006 interface-contract violation. Interface names are read from `ADR-006-module-boundaries.md`, and its four conditions are checked for every cross-module implementation discovered; the dependency that must exist without the implementation is counted only from classes other than the implementation.
- **Validation:** `npm run test:ui` passed 38/38. The full `./mvnw -B test` passed after the review fixes: 762 tests (757 before A-02 and its 5), 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped, BUILD SUCCESS. No A-02 commit has been made.

## Evidence for Task A-03 (Platform, 23 September 2026)

The task is two commits: `R4` in the shared mail and SMTP services first, the class moves after it.

### Part 1 — `R4`: the shared mail and SMTP services take the verified actor and the recipient from their caller

- **Change:** `SmtpConfigurationService` stops looking an Admin up. `saveDraft`, `testDraft`, `activate`, `setupStatus` and `history` take the identity id the caller has already verified, and `testDraft` takes the recipient address as well; the `AccountService` dependency is removed. `SmtpController` keeps the verification (`accounts.requireActiveAdminId`) and resolves the recipient from the verified identity (`accounts.requireIdentityById(...).email()`) — the same address the service resolved before, still never from request input. `AdminSettingsController` already verified the Admin before asking setup state and history, and `SmtpWarningAdvice` reads only `hasActiveConfiguration`.
- **Behavior:** an unknown or inactive actor is still refused with `Admin not found`, now by the caller; the probe still sends to the retained address of the signed-in Admin; the draft, test, activation and retirement sequence is unchanged.
- **Cycle list:** one line deleted, none added. `A SmtpConfigurationService AccountService R4` is gone, because the reference it recorded no longer exists. Allowances 31 → 30. The `P` rows and the `R7`/`R10` allowance rows belong to part 2.
- **Test changes:** 24 `testDraft` call sites across 19 test files gain the recipient they already passed as the draft's From address; argument-only, per A.7.
- **Changed assertion lines (A.7):** three lines of `SmtpIntegrationTest` change receiver and nothing else — `assertThatThrownBy(() -> smtpService.history(404L))`, `assertThatThrownBy(() -> smtpService.testDraft(404L, 404L))` and `assertThatThrownBy(() -> smtpService.activate(404L, 404L))` become `assertThatThrownBy(() -> accountService.requireActiveAdminId(404L))`, because the actor check those lines provoke moved from the service to its caller. The expectations chained to them (`isInstanceOf(IllegalArgumentException.class)`, `hasMessage("Admin not found")`) keep the values they stated. One further line of the same file, `assertThatThrownBy(() -> smtpService.testDraft(draftId, adminId))`, changes only its argument. No other file changes an assertion, and no test file changes its number of assertion calls.
- **Evidence:** `./mvnw -B test` 762 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped, BUILD SUCCESS; `npm run test:ui` 38/38; `git diff --check` clean. Java 25.0.4.1 (Temurin), Docker 29.7.2, PostgreSQL 18.4 through Testcontainers, nothing else running alongside.
- **Impact analysis:** `SmtpConfigurationService` upstream LOW, three importing files; `testDraft` upstream UNKNOWN as an ambiguous name (two declarations), LOW once disambiguated, and confirmed by text search to 26 call sites. UNKNOWN was not read as an all-clear.
- **Change analysis:** `node .gitnexus/run.cjs detect-changes --scope all --repo .` reports 23 files, 53 symbols, 18 affected execution flows, risk level **critical**, and no partial or truncated result. The level is the shared SMTP writer being touched: the flows it names (`SaveDraft`, `Activate`, `Test → Email`) are exactly the ones this commit changes, and the whole flow is covered by `SmtpIntegrationTest`, `IntegrationExternalTransactionIntegrationTest`, `SmtpOnboardingWebIntegrationTest` and the sixteen setup call sites, all of which pass. The risk is reported here rather than treated as resolved.

Keyed by simple name, a row survives a move and goes stale only when the class reaches its module. ## Evidence for Task A-04 (identity, 23 September 2026)

- **Placement.** Every class of `feature.account` is now `feature.identity`, the nine internship classes included, and `SmtpController` joined them. The boundary list lost the `account` package row and the `SmtpController` row; nothing was added. The references from `AccountController` to `project` stay as allowances until A-10 and A-11.
- **Two defects found on review.** `SmtpController` had been moved into the identity directory while its `package` line still read `feature.integration.controller`, which compiles but left the class outside every module; its web test was still in the old test package. Both were corrected, and a check over every source now shows no file whose package declaration differs from its directory.
- **The E2E clock assertion changed, which `TST-011` requires to be recorded.** `E2eProfileIntegrationTest` asserted the `e2e` clock was within five seconds of its anchor. That clock is anchored when its bean is created, so the distance from the anchor measures the startup left after that moment, which bean order decides; renaming `account` to `identity` changed that order and the distance grew past five seconds. Product behaviour did not change: the clock stayed anchored at the configured instant and advancing. The assertion now bounds the distance by the JVM uptime, which no machine speed or bean order can beat, and adds that the clock advances by the same amount as real time across a 200 ms wait, within 50 ms. It is stricter than the window it replaces: it pins both the anchor and the rate.
- **Validation.** `./mvnw -B test`: 762 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors. `npm run test:ui`: 38/38. `node scripts/module-boundaries.cjs`: 311 assigned, 0 problems. `git diff --check`: clean. Apart from the clock assertion above, no assertion changed.

## Evidence for Task A-05 (attendance and the policy boundary, 23 September 2026)

- **What changed.** `AttendanceRecordEntity` and `LeaveRequestDayEntity` keep the policy version as an identifier instead of a JPA association, and `toDomain` receives the `AttendancePolicy` record. The calendar contract gained reads that return the policy for a set of version identifiers, the calendar events of a date range, the policy timeline, and the locked policy version a quota decision needs. The schema does not change.
- **The four services.** `AttendanceApplicationService`, `AttendanceCorrectionApplicationService`, `LeaveApplicationService` and `AttendanceReportQueryService` no longer name a policy or calendar entity or repository. Each collects the version identifiers a request needs and reads them in one call before it loops; the single-record paths read once and are not reached from a loop. The classes that still name those entities are the calendar classes themselves, which A-06 places.
- **Found on review.** The work did not compile: two imports were missing, `AttendancePolicy` in `AttendanceRecordEntity` and `FetchType` in `LeaveRequestDayEntity`. The incremental build hid it, and the Lombok errors that then appeared across `identity` were a symptom of the aborted compile, not a second defect. A clean build is what catches this, and it is run from here on after every task that moves or renames packages.
- **Validation.** `./mvnw -B clean test`: 762 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors. `npm run test:ui`: 38/38. `git diff --check`: clean. One assertion line changed by wrapping only; no expected value moved.

## Evidence for Task A-10 (internship and R3, 24 September 2026)

- **Impact and RED evidence.** GitNexus reported HIGH risk for `findAdministrationViewsByFilter` across four Admin directory processes and CRITICAL risk for `findEligibleInternOptions` across nine Project and reporting processes before the edit. The two PLAN A.7 tests first passed against the original joins. Removing the Student Code side then made the directory-union test lose its profile-only match; sorting display names in Java made the collation test return the wrong order. Both deliberate mutations were restored before implementation.
- **Composition.** `InternshipService` now composes the Admin directory as the union of identity name/email matches and Intern Student Code matches, deduplicated and ordered by account ID. Eligibility and due activation are intersections of batched identity and profile reads. Eligibility uses the positions returned by the database for display name and Student Code, treats identical names as tied, and never compares names in Java. Each composition performs a bounded number of module reads independent of row count.
- **Placement and routes.** The nine planned classes, the internship methods formerly on `AccountService`, and the Admin account screens now belong to `feature.internship`; tests moved with their classes and every package declaration matches its directory. Identity lifecycle routes remain in `IdentityAccountController` on their existing paths. Production `identity` has no reference to `internship`, and no old identity FQCN for a moved type remains.
- **Invariant and test rule.** A-09, the directory, eligibility, due-date, lifecycle and lock-order focused set passed 40/40. Across the final `src/test` diff the assertion-call total increases by exactly two, the two A-10 tests named by PLAN A.7. Ten existing web assertions moved unchanged with the split identity controller; all other changed assertion lines change only a receiver or constructor/mock argument, with expected values preserved.
- **Boundary list.** Nine placement entries, ten `R3` allowances and the now-stale `AccountService -> InternshipLifecycleGuard` `R1` allowance were deleted: 20 entries in total, plus the obsolete placement header. No entry was added. Three `R1` allowances remain for A-11, all from the internship account controller to project.
- **Clean-build findings.** The first clean suite ran 767 tests and exposed one stale allowance, three reflected constructor signatures, and two web-slice mocks omitted by the focused set; their eight context errors were consequences of the two missing mocks. These were corrected only as A.7 permits. The failed UI run exposed the analyzer's missing `feature.internship` package mapping; `moduleOfClass` had LOW impact and received only that mapping, while A-12 still owns retirement of the analyzer.
- **Graph review.** After refreshing the GitNexus index and staging the complete snapshot, change detection covered 77 files and 388 symbols across 138 execution flows. It reported CRITICAL aggregate risk, expected because account creation, lifecycle, eligibility and all consumers move together; the listed flows are the A-10 create, edit, complete, withdraw, attendance, project and reporting paths. The result was neither partial nor truncated. Repository-wide flow extraction remains capped and Windows FTS remains unavailable, so the clean suites and explicit package/reference audits remain the primary evidence.
- **Final validation.** `./mvnw -B clean test` on Java 25.0.4.1 passed 767 tests with 0 failures, 0 errors and 0 skipped. `npm run test:ui` on Node 24.16.0 passed 38/38. Package/path verification, `git diff --check`, the structure test and the boundary-cycle test pass. Files under `src/` changed; no schema or dependency changed, and the documentation-link contract found no broken link.

## Evidence for Task A-11 (R1 readiness interface, 24 September 2026)

- **Authorship.** Codex wrote the interface, its implementation and the call-site changes, and stopped before recording evidence. Claude finished the task: the RED run, the validation and this record. An independent review session then found three problems in that work, which Claude corrected with the maintainer's approval (below). No impact analysis is recorded for Codex's first edits; the maintainer accepted that gap, and the change detection below covers the whole diff.
- **Interface.** `internship` declares `InternshipLifecycleReadiness` and calls it from completion, withdrawal and the account page; `project` implements it in `ProjectInternshipLifecycleReadiness`. `preview` keeps the predicate of the former `ProjectQueryService.internshipLifecycleGuard` and `lockForTerminalAction` that of the former `ProjectService.lockedInternshipLifecycleGuard`, unchanged. Completion and withdrawal no longer accept a guard from their caller, and `ProjectService` no longer orchestrates them. `ADR-006` names the interface.
- **Lock order.** Unchanged: account, then Intern profile, then Project. `InternshipService.lockedAccountMutationEligibility` locks the Intern profile right after the accounts, before `lockForTerminalAction` locks the Projects. Claude's first version of this record and of the interface Javadoc said account, Project, profile; review showed that wrong, and Codex's original Javadoc is restored.
- **Refusal response.** When a Project disappears between the membership read and the route read, the former `internshipLifecycleGuard` threw `ProjectAccessDeniedException`, which the account page answered with 404. The first version of `preview` threw `IllegalStateException`, which the page does not catch, so the same race became a 500. `preview` now throws `IllegalArgumentException`, which the page maps to 404, as PLAN A.1 requires. `lockForTerminalAction` keeps `IllegalStateException`, as the former `lockedInternshipLifecycleGuard` did. The account controller catches one exception type where it caught two.
- **Impact.** GitNexus impact on `lockForTerminalAction`: LOW, callers `InternshipService.completeInternship` and `withdrawInternship`, then `AccountController`. On `preview` it returned UNKNOWN ("Target not found"), and on its caller `internshipLifecycleReadiness` CRITICAL with 32 flows whose names (`Leadership → Id`, `ApproveExit → GetDisplayName`) do not touch it. Text search bounded the real reach: one production path, the account detail page (`AccountController` → `InternshipService.internshipLifecycleReadiness` → `preview`).
- **RED evidence.** With the unfinished-Task count of `lockForTerminalAction` multiplied by zero, the new test `terminalCompletionRejectsUnfinishedTaskWithoutLeadershipAndPreservesProfile` failed with `Expected java.lang.IllegalStateException to be thrown, but nothing was thrown`: completion went through. The file was restored from a copy, byte-identical, and the test then passed 1/1.
- **Test rule.** The two assertions of `InternshipLifecycleIntegrationTest` that fed a guard are removed, as PLAN A.7 allows. The other changed assertion lines change only a receiver or argument order (`projects` to `internships`), with expected values preserved; the one added test is the refusal test of A.7.
- **Cycle test correction.** `ModuleBoundaryCycleTest` marked an `ADR-006` interface as called by its declaring module only after skipping every reference inside one module, so the mark could never be set and no real interface could satisfy the third condition. The mark now runs before that skip. This corrects the boundary test this part created; no assertion or expected value changes, and the maintainer accepted it here rather than as an A.7 amendment.
- **Boundary list.** The three remaining `R1` allowances were deleted and nothing was added; `module-boundaries.tsv` holds only its header, so both lists of the cycle test are empty. The cycle test checks the four `ADR-006` conditions for the new interface.
- **Graph review.** After refreshing the index, GitNexus change detection saw 18 files and 34 symbols (35 in the reviewer's later run; the count follows the index state), LOW risk, with no partial or truncated flag. Zero affected processes is not proof of no impact, since flow extraction is capped; the clean suites remain the primary evidence.
- **Final validation.** `./mvnw -B clean test` on Java 25.0.4.1 passed 768 tests with 0 failures, 0 errors and 0 skipped, one more than A-10. `npm run test:ui` passed 38/38. `git diff --check` is clean. No schema or dependency changed.

## Evidence for Task A-12 (closing part A, 24 September 2026)

- **Authorship.** Claude did A-12; an independent review precedes the commit. The maintainer answered that they did not know which option was best on two points, the cycle-test correction and the list-file history, and left them to Claude. Both are recorded below as Claude's choices, for review.
- **Failure paths, seen on real code.** Temporary classes prefixed `ZzA12` were added and removed around each run; the working tree was compared with its prior state and `ADR-006` restored byte-identical from a copy.
  - Three interfaces, each breaking one `ADR-006` condition, produced exactly `ZzA12UnlistedPort: not listed in ADR-006`, `ZzA12UncalledPort: not called by declaring module` and `ZzA12NoDependencyPort: no independent dependency`. The last is declared in `notification` and implemented in `internship`, which refers to no `notification` type.
  - A reference into `config`: no `config` type is public, so Java refuses a real one when compiling. A nested class named `TimeConfiguration` inside an `identity` class made the name-based scan report `ZzA12ConfigReference -> TimeConfiguration`.
  - An upward reference from `identity` to `project` was reported as `ZzA12UpwardReference -> ProjectService`.
  - The cycle path cannot fail alone on real code: a cycle needs an upward reference, which the unallowed-reference assertion refuses first. It is seen only through `rejectsAnArtificialCycle`, as in A-02.
- **Cycle test correction.** The second `ADR-006` condition, every implementation in another module, could not fail on real code: the scan recorded only implementations outside the declaring module, so an interface with one implementation inside and one outside passed. The scan now records the module of every implementation, while an interface still counts as a cross-module contract only when some implementation lies outside its module. A temporary interface implemented in both `internship` and `project` then failed with exactly `ZzA12MixedPort: implementation is in declaring module`. This corrects the boundary test's own logic, as in A-11; no assertion or expected value changes. PLAN A.7 does not name it.
- **List file history.** Since `3b679d0` (A-02) the file lost 122 lines and gained 43. The 43 are rewrites, not new entries: 42 in `68ceece` re-key placement rows from fully qualified to simple class names, same class and module, as the A-03 evidence records; 1 in `502169e` re-adds an identical allowance in another position. A script matched every added row to a deleted row it rewrites. No violation was added after A-02, but the literal wording of A-12, "adds no line", is not met.
- **Analyzer.** The cycle check of `scripts/module-boundaries.cjs` is retired: the code scan, the rule and prose edges, the graph, the cycle and composition-root reports, `edges.tsv` and `--merge-internship` are removed, 675 lines to 363; the script now refuses an unsupported option such as `--merge-internship` with exit 1, instead of ignoring it. Rule assignment and the concept scan remain; the layers now come from the "Computed layers" table of `ADR-006`, and a module missing there fails the run, seen by deleting the `reporting` row and then restoring `ADR-006` byte-identical. `rule-map.tsv`, `concept-scan.tsv` and the concept-scan report are byte-identical before and after. GitNexus impact on the removed functions: LOW. The JS contract test needed no change: its assertions read only the output that is preserved.
- **Structure tests.** `ModuleBoundaryCycleTest` 5/5; `LayerStructureTest`, `AttendanceLayerStructureTest`, `ReportingArchitectureTest` and `AttendanceAndTaskWorkSeparationTest` pass.
- **Graph review.** On the A-12 diff: 2 files, 20 symbols, LOW risk, 0 affected processes. Over the whole part against `2945975`: 301 files, the same 301 git reports, 330 symbols, 656 of the 755 indexed processes, CRITICAL risk. The part moved nearly every package, so nearly every flow passes through a moved class; that is the expected set. No task listed its expected processes beforehand, so this is an argument from the scope of the part, not a comparison against a list. Neither result carried a partial or truncated flag.
- **End-to-end.** Playwright 1.62.1 against the application on the `e2e` profile (`LAB_E2E_START_INSTANT=2026-09-24T01:00:00Z`, `E2E_BUSINESS_DATE=2026-09-24`), a disposable PostgreSQL 18.4 container on port 55433 and the development Mailpit. The first run passed 4 and skipped the 2 report journeys for want of `E2E_EMAIL`; rerun with the Intern the critical journey had created, `intern-1790206952146@e2e.test`, both passed. Six of six journeys pass, as in A-01. The container was removed afterwards.

## Evidence for Task D-01 (business SQL behind the data-access layer, 24 September 2026)

- **Authorship.** Codex wrote `BusinessSqlBoundaryTest`, the eight JPQL deletes in `ProjectRepository` and the calls to them in `ProjectService`, then stopped when its quota ran out, with no RED run, impact analysis or gate recorded. Claude ran the checks below, corrected three comments that still said native, and wrote this record; an independent review precedes the commit.
- **Impact, after the edits.** None was recorded before Codex's edits, a departure from `AGENTS.md` accepted after the independent review of 24 September 2026, as at A-13: both changed methods were private to `ProjectService`, so their only caller was `ProjectService#delete`, and `ProjectServiceIntegrationTest` passed unchanged. Run afterwards: `deleteProjectRows` LOW (2 impacted), `nativeDelete` LOW (3), `ProjectRepository` LOW (17, 0 affected processes). A text search shows both methods called only inside `ProjectService`.
- **RED.** With `ProjectService` and `ProjectRepository` restored to `570d9ef` and the new test kept, `BusinessSqlBoundaryTest` failed with exactly `["ProjectService.java:1590 createNativeQuery"]`. Codex's versions were then put back unchanged.
- **Review fixes.** The independent review found two forms the scanner let pass: a method reference `em::createNativeQuery`, and a text block with an odd number of `"` that hid code after a later `//`. The `createNative` rule no longer requires `(`, and text blocks are tracked. Both forms, put in temporary production classes, were then reported as `ZzD01MethodRefProbe.java:8 createNative*` and `ZzD01TextBlockProbe.java:9 createNative*`; the probes were removed. RED against `570d9ef` rerun with the fixed scanner failed with exactly `["ProjectService.java:1590 createNative*"]`. The eight repository methods were re-indented to the file's four spaces, and the comment delete written as a text block. A second review found that both annotation rules missed an annotation written with its package, such as `@org.hibernate.annotations.Formula` or `@jakarta.persistence.NamedNativeQuery`; they now accept an optional package. Two probes, put in temporary production classes, were then reported as `ZzD01QualifiedFormulaProbe.java:4 Hibernate SQL fragment annotation` and `ZzD01QualifiedNamedNativeProbe.java:3 native query annotation`, and removed; RED against `570d9ef` again failed with exactly `["ProjectService.java:1590 createNative*"]`.
- **Behavior unchanged.** The eight statements become eight JPQL bulk deletes in the same child-to-parent order, still after `flush` and `clear`. No entity carries `@Where`, `@Filter`, `@SQLRestriction` or `@SoftDelete`, so JPQL deletes the rows the SQL deleted, and the eight entities map the eight tables. `ProjectServiceIntegrationTest#ownerCanDeleteAPlannedProjectAndItsOwnedRows` creates rows in all eight tables, an invitation, an exit request, a Task, a comment and a work log among them, and asserts each is gone; it passes unchanged.
- **Test changes against A.7.** `BusinessSqlBoundaryTest` is added; no existing test file changed.
- **Validation.** `BusinessSqlBoundaryTest`, `LayerStructureTest`, `ModuleBoundaryCycleTest` and `ProjectServiceIntegrationTest`: 23 tests, 0 failures. `./mvnw -B clean test` on Java 25.0.4.1 with Docker: `Tests run: 770, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0` and `BUILD SUCCESS`, one more than 769, the new test; a first full run was cut off when the machine shut down and was rerun whole. After the review fixes, `./mvnw -B clean test` again gave `Tests run: 770, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0` and `BUILD SUCCESS`. `npm run test:ui`: 38/38. `git diff --check` is clean. After the second review's fix, `./mvnw -B clean test` gave `Tests run: 770, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0` and `BUILD SUCCESS`, `npm run test:ui` passed 38/38, and `git diff --check` is clean.
- **End-to-end.** Playwright 1.62.1 against the application of this branch on the `e2e` profile (`LAB_E2E_START_INSTANT=2026-09-24T01:00:00Z`, `E2E_BUSINESS_DATE=2026-09-24`), a freshly created disposable PostgreSQL 18.4 container on port 55433 and the development Mailpit. The application read the local development settings file through `LAB_DEV_ENV_FILE`, with the database overridden to the disposable container; the agent did not open that file. The first run passed 4 and skipped the 2 report journeys for want of credentials; rerun with the Intern the critical journey had created, both passed. Six of six. The application was stopped and the container removed. It ran before the reviewer's fixes; the fixes change only the scanner, the formatting of one query and documents, so it was not rerun.
- **Graph review.** No expected-process list was written before the edits. Before the review fixes, `analyze --index-only` then `detect-changes --scope all` reported 3 tracked files, 13 symbols, 4 affected processes and MEDIUM risk: the Project deletion flow reaching four of the new repository deletes. After the last edit the same pair reported 5 tracked files, 6 symbols, 0 affected processes and LOW risk, resolving the changes only to the `ProjectRepository` interface and the `ProjectService` class rather than to their methods; the deletion flow still passes through the changed code. Neither run carried a partial or truncated flag, and the new test file is untracked and not counted.

## Evidence for Task B-01

- **Scope and authorship (25–26 September 2026).** Branch `work/fix/platform/authorization-matrix`, from `main` `c67d079`. Codex built `AuthorizationMatrixIntegrationTest`, `open-matrix-cells.tsv` and `unbuilt-operations.tsv` in stages under the reviewer's rulings. Claude reviewed each stage, consolidated this evidence, moved the temporary logs out of every repository worktree, and ran the full suite and the graph check. No file under `src/main` changed.
- **Design.** The test reads the §5.2 table of the Authorization SPEC at run time, and its registry, one entry per capability label, must equal the table's 36 rows. Each capability has one or more service probes, run on PostgreSQL 18.4 through Testcontainers under the `test` profile, with each actor placed in the scope its column names. A control runs first on the same fixture: the first actor the row grants or, for the all-`No` row `Start another member's Task or mark it DONE`, the Task's assignee, whom the row `Change the status of one's own assigned Task` grants the same transition. A failed control is a setup error, never a refusal. Probes run in a transaction that is rolled back, except the leave-withdrawal probe, which commits its setup with unique identifiers because `LeaveApplicationService.cancel` runs in `REQUIRES_NEW`.
- **Refusal exceptions.** `AccessDeniedException` is an explicit authorization denial. `ProjectAccessDeniedException` and `TaskNotFoundException` are non-disclosing scope denials; `TaskNotFoundException` counts because `AUTH-002` requires refusing an inaccessible record without revealing that it exists, and the probe's granted control establishes that the record exists. `IllegalArgumentException` counts only with the exact messages `Admin not found` or `An active Admin is required`, the two current Admin lookup guards; B-02 routes these through the policy. Any other exception is a fixture or test error.
- **Ledgers.** `open-matrix-cells.tsv` lists each cell whose behavior differs from §5.2, with the task that fixes it; from this commit on it only loses lines, and plan section B.10 requires it empty. `unbuilt-operations.tsv` lists each operation §5.2 names that the code does not yet offer, with the plan that builds it; it only loses lines, as that plan adds the operation's probe. A capability with no probe must map to listed unbuilt operations, or the test fails naming the row.
- **Impact.** `TaskService.changeStatus` at 2026-09-25 11:50:20 UTC: LOW, two direct callers, one process, a lower bound because some call sites were untyped. Before the temporary calendar RED, `CalendarApplicationService.createManual`: LOW, one direct caller, one process, a lower bound; the index was that of another local worktree at `8587d68`, and the service file is identical between `0c98651` and `c67d079`. The new test helpers (`isAuthorizationDenial`, `inRolledBackFixture`, `fixTaskFor`, `probes`) returned UNKNOWN; a text search finds only local test call sites.
- **Final focused run.** `.\mvnw.cmd -Dtest=AuthorizationMatrixIntegrationTest test`, Java 25.0.4.1, Maven Wrapper 3.9.16, `test` profile, PostgreSQL 18.4 through Testcontainers: 1 test passed; 127 exercised cells + 17 unbuilt cells = all 144; 8 mismatches, equal to the 8 lines of the open-cell ledger.
- **Open cells (8; fix task last):**
  - Change the status of one's own assigned Task (any `TSK-007` transition) | Owning Mentor | ALLOWED | B-02
  - Start another member's Task or mark it `DONE` | Owning Mentor | ALLOWED | B-02
  - View per-member Project hours | Admin | DENIED | B-03
  - View per-member Project hours | Active member / assignee | ALLOWED | B-03
  - View and export the Project/Task report (`RPT-005`) | Admin | DENIED | B-03
  - View and export the Daily Project Work Report (`RPT-011`) | Admin | DENIED | B-03
  - View own attendance for a date (`RPT-015`) | Admin | ALLOWED | B-03
  - View own attendance for a date (`RPT-015`) | Owning Mentor | ALLOWED | B-03

  The Admin denials move behind the policy in B-03, as `ADR-005` requires, and the matrix grants Admin read-only access (`D1`). If B-03 finds a report cannot serve an Admin without changing the reporting feature itself, it stops and reports.
- **Unbuilt operations (10; rule and building plan):**
  - Admin deactivation of a `PENDING_ACTIVATION` account | `ACC-028` | Account lifecycle plan
  - Admin reinstatement transitions from `DEACTIVATED` | `ACC-029` | Account lifecycle plan
  - Edit Project details | `AUTH-012` | Project lifecycle plan (to be written)
  - Cancel a `PLANNED` Project | `PRJ-023` | Project lifecycle plan (to be written)
  - Cancel an `ACTIVE` Project | `PRJ-023` | Project lifecycle plan (to be written)
  - Leader block, unblock or reopen of another member's Task | `TSK-023`, `TSK-025` | Task management plan, after part C creates `task_status_transitions` (Authorization plan B.7)
  - Submit an attendance exception request | `ATT-024` | Attendance plan C-04, after part C creates the exception request model
  - Decide leave, correction, or attendance exception; amend or reverse that decision | `ATT-024` | Attendance exception plan, after part C adds the responsible Mentor (`intern_profiles.responsible_mentor_user_id`, V3)
  - Ask to reopen a finalized attendance period | `ATT-022` | Period finalization plan, after part C creates the reopen request data
  - Approve or reject a request to reopen a finalized attendance period | `ATT-022` | Period finalization plan, after part C creates the reopen request data

  The 17 unbuilt cells are the four rows with no probe (Project cancellation, the `ATT-024` decision and both `ATT-022` reopen rows) and the Current Leader cell of `TSK-023`.
- **RED.** In the first stage, a temporary change of a registry key failed the registry-to-spec assertion, and a temporary `AccessDeniedException` in `CalendarApplicationService.createManual` failed the exact permitted Admin calendar cell. On the final test, each run restored afterwards: (1) deleting the `RPT-005` / Admin ledger line failed the exact-ledger assertion, 8 observed against 7 listed; (2) forcing the permitted `View all Projects/tasks/progress` / Owning Mentor cell to DENIED failed and printed that exact cell; (3) removing the first capability's probe failed the registry-to-spec assertion, naming `Manage accounts/global roles at creation`; (4) removing the unbuilt mapping of the probe-less Project cancellation row failed the row's "empty probe list" assertion. The unmodified test then passed.
- **Gates.** `npm run test:ui`: 38/38 (Node 24.16.0, npm 11.13.0). `git diff --check` passes. `./mvnw -B clean test` on Java 25.0.4.1 with Docker: `Tests run: 782, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0` and `BUILD SUCCESS`, one more than the 781 on `main`, the new matrix test.
- **Graph after the last edit.** This worktree has no GitNexus runner; the runner of another local worktree was used with `--repo .`. After the last edit, `analyze --index-only` indexed 8,019 nodes, 26,120 edges, 339 clusters and 681 flows, and `detect-changes --scope all` reported 1 tracked file, 3 symbols, 0 affected processes and LOW risk, all in this file. Its truncation warning concerns the flow count of the whole index, not this diff; the new test files are untracked and not counted.

## Evidence for Task B-02 (26 September 2026)

- **Scope, snapshot and deviation.** On 26 September 2026 the maintainer decided that B-02, B-03, B-04 and B-06 are stacked on `work/fix/platform/authorization-matrix`, one commit per task and one pull request when part B is done; this departs from `OPS-019`, which starts each task from `main`. No branch switch, commit, push or merge was made. Before the first edit, the tracked diff and all 9 then-untracked files were copied to a temporary directory outside every repository. The snapshot preserves the original B-02 changes for recovery. Its machine path is reported in the completion message and deliberately omitted here.
- **Design.** The structured `authorization-matrix.tsv` cells accept only exact `Yes`/`No` plus the generic `scope`, `not` and `edges` predicates; malformed syntax, duplicate/unknown keys, missing capabilities and unknown actor columns fail catalogue construction. If any granted cell in a capability row defines transition edges, every granted cell in that row must define edges. A `not=` predicate denies when the request has no record state. The `BLOCK_UNBLOCK_REOPEN_TASK` Current Leader cell is limited to the same five explicit transition edges as the Owning Mentor cell, within `ACTIVE` scope. The policy consumes only `AuthorizationCapability` and `AuthorizationRequest`; the record-owning module resolves scope and supplies state facts. Attendance authorization validates actor identity/role and target existence/type, derives columns, then calls the policy once. Task status mutation retains `TaskNotFoundException` for the Mentor denial. The layout delegates the report-link decision to the same policy.
- **Assertion changes.** `AuthorizationPolicyTest#catalogueMatchesEveryCapabilityAndCellInTheSpec` checks the exact structured predicates resolved from each normative §5.2 condition. New cases prove that `not=DONE` denies with a missing record state and that a transition row is rejected when any granted cell omits `edges=`; other cases check scope/state evaluation and malformed cells. Both review regression tests were first run before implementation and failed for the expected missing-state allowance and missing-edges validation. `TaskMutationBoundaryTest#owningMentorCannotSetAnUnlistedTaskStatus` replaces the mocked policy invocation check with the real catalogue and an assertion that TODO→IN_PROGRESS is denied without mutating or saving the Task (`AUTH-012`, `TSK-023`). `TaskCreationIntegrationTest#owningMentorCannotStartAnotherMembersTask` replaces the prior success/status/notification assertions with the Task remaining TODO and no notification (§5.2 row `Start another member's Task or mark it DONE`, Owning Mentor `No`; `TSK-023`). `AttendanceReportQueryServiceAuthorizationTest` uses the real policy/catalogue and adds the active-Intern/other-Intern denial (`AUTH-012`, `RPT-004`); its inactive-Admin case now asserts target validation then policy denial. The report-page, dashboard and notification MVC slices load the actual policy/catalogue for the shared layout; navigation tests cover active, inactive and missing stored identity. No assertion was weakened.
- **Baseline impact.** A detached worktree at `62b79e4` was analyzed with `E:/labtimesheet-business-sql/.gitnexus/run.cjs --repo .`, then removed; `git worktree list` confirmed its removal. At 2026-09-26 09:38:02 UTC, `TaskService.changeStatus` had LOW risk, 2 impacted sites and one process (`TaskController.changeStatus`); the result was a lower bound because 3 receiver-typing call sites were dropped. At 09:37:50 UTC, `AttendanceReportQueryService.authorize` had HIGH risk, 5 impacted nodes and 3 flows (`report`, `attendancePdf`, `attendanceXlsx`) across 2 modules. The authorization classes do not exist at that baseline, so their impact there is UNKNOWN. Current-worktree pre-edit impact at 09:44:59 UTC for `changeStatus` was LOW/lower-bound; `authorize` at 09:44:46 UTC was HIGH with the same 3 report flows. A refreshed impact at 10:25:47 UTC again marked `authorize` HIGH: 5 impacted, 1 direct caller, 3 flows and 2 modules. `authorizationRequest` was UNKNOWN/0 at 10:19:22 UTC; text search confirmed its `@ModelAttribute` and the layout property reference. FTS was unavailable and GitNexus warned that entry points and whole flows were dropped, so absent flows are not evidence of no impact.
- **Departure: edits before impact, the third in part B.** The B-02 code that existed before the review was written before any impact analysis ran. What stands in for it is the impact at the pre-edit baseline `62b79e4` recorded above; the new `platform/authorization` classes do not exist there. The reviewer accepted this on 26 September 2026; from B-03 on, a change made without a recorded pre-edit impact is reverted.
- **HIGH impact accepted.** `AttendanceReportQueryService.authorize` returned HIGH before its edit. Its three flows are the Attendance report page and its PDF and XLSX exports, the entry points this task guards. The reviewer accepted the risk on 26 September 2026 on the strength of the attendance authorization tests and the full suite, and told the maintainer.
- **Tool side effect.** `gitnexus analyze` rewrote the GitNexus section of `AGENTS.md` and inserted one into `CLAUDE.md` during this task. Both files were restored to their `62b79e4` content and are not part of B-02; later runs used `analyze --index-only`.
- **Review-round impact.** Before the G1/G2 edits, `AuthorizationCatalogue` impact at 2026-09-26 11:18:05 UTC was LOW: 2 direct sites (`AuthorizationPolicy` and `loadDefault`), 0 processes, 1 module. `structuredCell` impact at 11:19:38 UTC was UNKNOWN/0; text search confirmed its call from `catalogueRow`, so the empty graph result was not treated as proof of no callers.
- **RED evidence.** R1: temporarily removing the Task policy gate made `TaskMutationBoundaryTest#owningMentorCannotSetAnUnlistedTaskStatus` fail because `changeStatus` was called; the matrix reported 8 mismatches, including exactly the two B-02 Mentor cells plus six B-03 cells. R2: temporarily removing the attendance policy call made `activeInternCannotReadAnotherInternAttendanceThroughTheRealPolicy` fail because no exception was thrown; the active Intern could read another Intern's report. R3: temporarily setting only the Admin cell of `ATTENDANCE_REPORT` to `No` removed the Admin dashboard link (`AdminDashboardWebTest#adminDashboardUsesAccountLifecycleSummariesOnly` failed on the missing “Attendance reports” text). For the matrix run, the temporary control actor was set to Owning Mentor because the default SPEC-order control selected the deliberately denied Admin. The result was 127 exercised, 17 unbuilt, 7 mismatches: exactly the prior six B-03 cells plus `View and export the Attendance report (RPT-004) / Admin = DENIED`. The test failed on the expected 6-versus-7 open-cell ledger. Both temporary files were restored; the permanent baseline matrix passes at 127/17/6.
- **Additional regression checks.** A missing stored identity first produced a focused `NullPointerException` RED in `AttendanceReportNavigationAdviceTest`; the fail-closed null guard passed that test and `AttendanceControllerTest`. The existing integration test `attendanceReportNormalizesTargetGuessesAcrossActorScopes` exposed a changed denial message for Intern guesses at non-Intern or absent targets; the role-preserving validation message was restored and that test passed. This keeps target validation before the single policy decision.
- **Final gates.** In the review round, `AuthorizationPolicyTest`, `AuthorizationMatrixIntegrationTest`, `TaskMutationBoundaryTest`, `TaskCreationIntegrationTest` and `AttendanceReportQueryServiceAuthorizationTest` passed 80/80, including the matrix at 127 exercised, 17 unbuilt and 6 open B-03 cells. `.\mvnw.cmd -B clean test` passed 852 tests, 0 failures/errors/skips (`BUILD SUCCESS`, 11:32); Maven Wrapper 3.9.16 resolved Java 25.0.4.1, and Testcontainers used PostgreSQL 18.4. `npm run test:ui` passed 38/38 on Node 24.16.0/npm 11.13.0, including documentation-link checks; `git diff --check` passed. After `analyze --index-only`, GitNexus 1.6.12 indexed 8,239 nodes, 26,537 edges, 357 clusters and 700 flows; FTS was unavailable and the whole-flow truncation warning remains. Final `detect-changes --scope all` reported 12 files, 29 symbols, 3 affected processes and MEDIUM risk, with no partial/truncated result flag; the listed processes are `ChangeStatus → Status`, `ChangeStatus → Actor` and `ChangeStatus → Project`.
- **State.** The two B-02 rows were removed from `open-matrix-cells.tsv`; the six remaining lines were the B-03 ledger before this step. B-02 was accepted and committed as `fb4f306` on 26 September 2026, without push. The 17 unbuilt cells remain.

- **Scope and mapping.** Applied authorization PLAN B.2, B.4, B.5, B.8, B.9 and B.10. Attendance history now asks the policy for `OWN_ATTENDANCE_DATE` when reading one's own history and `VIEW_INTERN_ATTENDANCE` for another Intern; Leave and Correction list, view and balance use `VIEW_INTERN_ATTENDANCE`; Leave and Correction submission, pending Leave edit and Leave withdrawal use `SUBMIT_ATTENDANCE_REQUEST` or `WITHDRAW_LEAVE_REQUEST` as mapped by §5.2. Controller display flags for Intern submission links use the policy. Actor identity, target type and record ownership stay resolved in Attendance. Decision methods and expiry/history helpers remain outside the change. The authorization PLAN records the explicit feature-to-row mapping.

Leave service method targets `list`, `view`, `balance`, `submit`, `edit`, `cancel` were UNKNOWN (`Target not found` with `--file`); correction `view` was UNKNOWN because multiple candidates matched, while correction `list` returned CRITICAL (159, lower-bound, one direct caller and receiver-typing callers dropped) and `submit` returned CRITICAL (298, lower-bound, two callers dropped). The method impact probes were run with `--file`; text search confirmed every changed Leave/Correction production caller is in `AttendanceRequestController` (including `submit` at line 186 and correction `submit` at line 317). Controller methods have no Java caller because they are HTTP entry points. Additional helper impact probes at 2026-09-26 13:37:28 UTC were UNKNOWN where the graph could not resolve the target; text search was used for those call sites. No impact number was treated as a clean zero.

| `PRJ-014` | Guard | `ProjectInvitationExitIntegrationTest.completionRequiresAllCurrentTasksDoneAndHistoryUsesRetainedRowsWithExactVisibility` covers the completion readiness, workflow resolution, notifications, and retained history. `ProjectEntityTest.aCompletedProjectCannotBeReopened` checks the aggregate's direct activation guard. New `ProjectServiceIntegrationTest.completedProjectCannotBeReactivatedThroughTheService` checks service refusal and unchanged `COMPLETED`; new `ProjectControllerTest.directActivationRequestForCompletedProjectIsRefused` checks a direct POST receives the generic 404 refusal. New `ProjectControllerTest.directRequestToMoveACompletedProjectBackToPlannedIsRefused` sends a direct POST to `/projects/{id}/plan`, the route a transition to `PLANNED` would take, and receives the generic 404 refusal without reaching `ProjectService`. No route or service operation moves a Project to `PLANNED`: `ProjectStatus.PLANNED` is written only as the initial value of `ProjectEntity.status`, so a `COMPLETED` Project cannot be moved back to `PLANNED`. |

- **Impact (26 September 2026, 18:15 +07:00).** The worktree began clean on `work/fix/project/task-creation-todo` at `a905c01`. This CLI version rejects `--repo` on `analyze`, so the worktree root was passed as its positional path; impact queries used `--repo .`. The first `TaskService#create` query was `UNKNOWN` because that spelling did not resolve; the file-qualified `create` method query returned LOW lower-bound risk, one direct TaskController caller and four call sites omitted because receiver types were unresolved. `TaskController` and its file-qualified `create` method returned `UNKNOWN` with no resolved upstream caller; the same four unresolved receiver sites remain a boundary. `CreateTaskCommand` and `TaskCreateForm` each matched both the record and its constructor, leaving the record result `UNKNOWN`; text search confirmed production binding/construction and existing service/test call sites. Text search also confirmed `POST /projects/{projectId}/tasks`. No HIGH or CRITICAL risk was returned; the UNKNOWN boundaries were checked by text search before editing.
- **RED.** After adding the optional status carriers and forwarding the web-bound value, `serviceRejectsEveryNonTodoRequestedInitialStatusBeforeWriting` failed on `IN_PROGRESS` with `Expecting code to raise a throwable.` The original service created the Task and notification instead of refusing it. Once the integration fixture initialized the installation state so the real MVC route passed `BootstrapAccessFilter`, a temporary removal of the new service guard made `webRouteRejectsEveryNonTodoRequestedInitialStatusWithoutWriting` fail with `Status expected:<200> but was:<302>`: the route redirected after creation instead of returning validation. `TaskService.java` SHA-256 before the temporary mutation and after restoration was identical: `0188EA33D9C9593C01A0AD8A2906CE61D9B9746D763D47D27A04424699FB4A47`. The first test-class run also exposed a test-fixture bootstrap 404 and an over-broad notification-count expectation; both test issues were corrected before the RED route rerun.

The new `pendingExitMemberCannotCreateOrReceiveANewTask` also passed before the behavior change for both the requester creating a self-Task and the Leader assigning to that pending-exit membership. The PLANNED/ACTIVE scenario now checks notification-count delta around self-creation so the Leader-assigned notification is not mistaken for a self-notification.
- **Implementation.** `TaskCreateForm` and `CreateTaskCommand` carry optional `TaskStatus initialStatus`; the existing constructors remain. The route forwards this value without adding a template input. `TaskService.create` rejects any non-null value other than `TODO` before title, access, persistence or notification work. The controller's existing validation handler redisplays `tasks/form` with a validation error. No authorization check or schema was changed.

After Review 1, `TaskCreationIntegrationTest`: 45 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped; its rejection coverage now executes 12 service combinations and 6 web submissions. ## Evidence for Task C-04 (26 September 2026)

- **Scope.** `V4__identity_account_contract.sql` tightens `ck_app_users_pending_password` (all password-hash rules, including a deactivated row's hash and activation pairing), `ck_app_users_activated_state` (activation by status only) and `ck_app_users_lock_timestamp` (a deactivated row keeps a lock only beside an activation timestamp), and adds `trg_app_users_identity_timestamps`, which compares old and new values with `IS DISTINCT FROM` and allows an activation timestamp to change only on `PENDING_ACTIVATION → ACTIVE` and a lock timestamp only on `ACTIVE → LOCKED` and `LOCKED → ACTIVE` (`DB-022`). V4 adds no table, column or foreign key, so §19.4 and the counts of `PlatformFoundationTest` are unchanged. Application code: `AppUser#deactivate` keeps the lock and accepts a pending account (`ACC-016`, `ACC-028`); `AppUser#reinstate` and `AccountService#reinstateAccount` choose `LOCKED`, `ACTIVE` or `PENDING_ACTIVATION` from the retained timestamps and restore no internship or Project state (`ACC-029`); `AccountService#deactivateAccount` invalidates a pending account's live activation and password-reset tokens; `POST /admin/accounts/{id}/reinstate` and the account page show deactivation for every non-deactivated account and reinstatement for a deactivated one. No other production code changed. Part B's `unbuilt-operations.tsv` lists `ACC-028` and `ACC-029` as unbuilt; whichever of part B and part C reaches `main` second deletes those two lines and exercises their matrix cells in the same change.
- **Tests.** Focused coverage ran 74 tests: `AccountContractIntegrationTest` (4: V3 stored-row migration, trigger refusals, 96-row `AC-DB-009` shape matrix with six lawful rows derived from `DB-022`, and all nine permitted transitions); `PendingAccountDeactivationIntegrationTest` (1, including invalidated activation/reset tokens, generic reset response, unchanged row counts and refusal of the old raw reset token after activation); `AccountReinstatementWebIntegrationTest` (1, A/B/C reinstatement, preserved password hash and lock timestamp, and retained withdrawal/membership history); `AppUserLifecycleTest` (2); `AccountWebIntegrationTest` (6, including Admin-only deactivation/reinstatement); `IdentityAccountControllerWebTest` (2); `AuthenticationWebIntegrationTest` (2, including `AC-ACC-022`); `SchemaExpansionProbeIntegrationTest` (51); and `SchemaExpansionBackfillIntegrationTest` (5). `ACC-030` and `AC-ACC-022` say a refused sign-in creates no session; the login page already holds an anonymous session for its CSRF token (`SEC-001`), so the tests read this as no authenticated session: the attempt is unauthenticated and the session holds no security context.
- **RED.** Reconstructed on a temporary worktree of `2ecdf95` (pre-C-04 code, no V4) with the new tests copied in: `AL-01` failed with the lock expected `2026-09-26T12:00:01Z` but `null`; pending deactivation failed with `IllegalStateException: Account cannot be deactivated in this state`; the Admin route test failed at its deactivated-status assertion; three of the four contract tests failed (shape matrix, timestamp rewrite accepted, Flyway not at version 4), while the permitted-transition test passed, as expected of a guard. Tests calling `reinstate` were not compiled there; their gap is the missing method. `AC-ACC-022` passed on the pre-C-04 code: `DatabaseUserDetailsService` already disables every account that is not `ACTIVE`, so `ACC-030` needed no production change (`AN-01`). The temporary worktree was removed.
- **Review RED.** F1 temporarily removed the V4 `CREATE TRIGGER trg_app_users_identity_timestamps` definition. `AccountContractIntegrationTest#schemaRefusesIdentityTimestampRewritesOutsideTheirTransitions` ran 1 test and failed as intended at both trigger-only statements with `Expecting actual not to be null` (no `SQLException`): `UPDATE app_users SET account_status='ACTIVE', locked_at=NULL, deactivated_at=NULL WHERE id=?` and `UPDATE app_users SET account_status='LOCKED', locked_at=?, deactivated_at=NULL WHERE id=?`. V4 SHA-256 before and after restoration: `609E1592E0B34C1F052DF25FFE62906DC256F1B2845B162EEDB95935D13A4E02` / `609E1592E0B34C1F052DF25FFE62906DC256F1B2845B162EEDB95935D13A4E02` (identical).
- **Review RED.** F3 temporarily removed `invalidateLiveTokens(targetUserId, TokenPurpose.PASSWORD_RESET, now)` from `AccountService#deactivateAccount`. `PendingAccountDeactivationIntegrationTest` ran 1 test and failed at the reset-token invalidation assertions with `Expecting actual not to be null` (lines 87 and 103), then proved the old raw reset token worked after activation (`Expecting value to be false but was true`, line 113) and changed the activated password hash (line 115). This is the B status/lock-state failure; C's reinstatement completed, then B's request failed before A was reinstated or any post-reinstatement assertions ran. AppUser SHA-256 before and after restoration: `FDE1140F64F3698F46C0B78B70A4244711EDE8D87987D259714FFE7D103D5F45` / `FDE1140F64F3698F46C0B78B70A4244711EDE8D87987D259714FFE7D103D5F45` (identical).

## Evidence for Task C-01 (25 September 2026)

- **Maintainer's decision, 25 September 2026.** C-01 itself reads and changes nothing. Beyond it, the maintainer decided to reuse the development database in `labtimesheet-postgres` (port `55432`) and to clean it before use, in this order: read and record, back up, drop, rebuild from V1 by Flyway; `labtimesheet-demo-postgres` is not to be touched.
- **Target and scope.** Used the running `labtimesheet-postgres` container (`postgres:18.4`, `127.0.0.1:55432`) and database/user `labtimesheet`. `labtimesheet-demo-postgres` was observed in `Exited` state and was not started, read or changed; its Flyway history and rows are therefore not recorded, and the C.5 finding of no quota above 4 covers only the two databases recorded here. The database owner was `labtimesheet`; `public` was owned by `pg_database_owner` with ACL `{pg_database_owner=UC/pg_database_owner,=U/pg_database_owner}`.
- **Before reset.** `flyway_schema_history` contained exactly `(1, baseline, -563737446, true)` and `(2, add task effort planning, 860065143, true)`, matching this branch's V1 and V2. The C.5 queries, run before the backup, found no `CANCELLED` leave requests (with or without `decided_at`), zero policy versions with quota above 4, no notification payloads for `NOT_REQUIRED` or `UNAVAILABLE`, and zero `DEACTIVATED` accounts. The independent review read the same tables from the backup: zero leave requests, two policy versions both with quota 3, two `UNAVAILABLE` notifications without payload, and four `ACTIVE` accounts. The schema contained the 24 application tables plus Flyway history.
- **End-to-end database.** `labtimesheet_e2e` in the same container, which the `e2e` profile migrates when the end-to-end suite runs, was read without change. Its `flyway_schema_history` holds the same two rows with the same checksums, installed on 22 September 2026. It holds no `CANCELLED` leave request (2 in total; both `APPROVED`), 2 policy versions with a maximum quota of 3, no notification in `NOT_REQUIRED` or `UNAVAILABLE` (18 in total; all `SENT`), and no `DEACTIVATED` account (9 in total). It was not backed up or reset; the maintainer's decision covers only the development database.
- **Backup.** Before reset, `pg_dump -Fc` created and `pg_restore -l` successfully listed the full database archive `labtimesheet-dev-c01-before-reset-20260925.dump`, kept outside the repository in the maintainer's local temporary directory and not committed (145,321 bytes; 285 TOC entries; PostgreSQL/pg_dump 18.4).
- **Reset and rebuild.** Dropped and recreated `public` as `pg_database_owner`, restoring `USAGE` to `PUBLIC`; the resulting owner and ACL matched the recorded values. The cascade removed 28 objects, including `btree_gist`, and the schema then had zero tables. Started the app from this worktree with `JAVA_HOME` set to Temurin 25.0.4.1 and `LAB_DEV_ENV_FILE` pointing to the ignored local env file in the original worktree (the file was not read or printed): `./mvnw spring-boot:run -Dspring-boot.run.profiles=dev`. Spring Boot logged `Started LabtimesheetApplication`; its process was then terminated, so Maven ended with `BUILD FAILURE` (exit code -1) after the start, not from a startup error. The rebuilt schema has 24 application tables and only these successful Flyway rows: `(1, baseline, -563737446, true)` and `(2, add task effort planning, 860065143, true)`. As expected from the baseline migration, it contains one `system_state`, one default attendance policy and five policy workdays; no pre-reset business rows were retained.
- **Evidence and limits.** The app launch compiled 294 production and 136 test source files; no Maven test suite, UI test, or E2E suite was run for C-01. Maven wrapper resolved Maven 3.9.16 on Java 25.0.4.1; Docker server was 29.7.2; PostgreSQL and pg_dump/pg_restore were 18.4. `git diff --check` passed. No files under `src/` were changed; this entry adds no documentation links.

## Evidence for Task T1-02 (26 September 2026)

- **Target and baseline.** Worktree `work/fix/project/task-definitions` began clean at `2a33181`. Before adding coverage, `./mvnw -B -Dtest=TaskCreationIntegrationTest test` passed 45 tests with 0 failures, 0 errors, and 0 skips against PostgreSQL 18.4 in Testcontainers. The final focused run after the test changes passed 46 tests. No production Java, schema, calendar module, or template file changed; no `.env` file was read and no database at port 55432 was used.
- **AC-TSK-002 / TSK-005.** Guard coverage proves Project boundary dates are accepted; dates before/after the Project and a current global day off are rejected on both create and edit. Rejected edits leave the existing null due date unchanged. The new later-day-off scenario creates the calendar event through the Admin calendar service after saving Tasks, then proves the saved date is unchanged and the Task-owned impact query returns only the non-deleted Task whose due date matches exactly.
- **AC-TSK-005 / TSK-010.** Guard coverage proves soft-deletion removes the Task from ordinary lists and progress, retains the database row, and returns the deleted Task in the owning Mentor's Project History with the Leader membership and exact deletion instant.
- **AC-TSK-006 / TSK-011 / TSK-012.** Guard coverage proves an active member, current Leader, and owning Mentor can append comments while the Project is not completed; the existing completed-Project refusal remains. Requests to edit or delete a comment have no route and return 404 without changing the retained comment.
- **AC-TSK-011 / TSK-004 / TSK-009 / TSK-019.** Guard coverage proves the Leader can reassign a member-created Task away and back; the creator cannot edit/delete while it is assigned away and regains those controls when it returns. Creator, `IN_PROGRESS` status, comment, 45-minute work log, final Leader assignment actor, and assignment time remain represented by the stored Task and history.
- `TSK-006` is not claimed complete: stored due dates stay unchanged and `TaskQueryService#dueDateImpacts` identifies the affected Tasks, but no calendar preview shows them yet. The query has no consumer, and the calendar module does not depend on the project module, so showing it needs a module boundary design under `ARC-005`; it is left to a Global calendar plan. This departs from the T1-02 row of `TASKS.md`, which named the preview.
- **RED and guards.** The initial 45-test run was green. The added assertions were guards for existing behavior and produced no production-behavior RED. Early post-edit attempts exposed test-authoring issues only (a wrong DTO accessor, a null-result JDBC mapper, an incorrect notification recipient expectation, a missing forecast fixture for a worked transfer, and an Admin fixture email mismatch); each was corrected, and the focused suite then passed 46/46. No authorization defect was found in the exercised T1-02 cases, so nothing was referred to part B's matrix ledger.
- **Runs and tools.** `./mvnw -B -Dtest=TaskCreationIntegrationTest test` passed 46 tests with 0 failures, 0 errors, and 0 skips. Full `./mvnw -B test` passed 852 tests with 0 failures, 0 errors, and 0 skips. `git diff --check` passed. No template changed, so `npm run test:ui` was not required or run. The wrapper resolved Maven 3.9.16 on Temurin Java 25.0.4.1; Testcontainers 2.0.5 used PostgreSQL 18.4, and Docker server was 29.7.2. Node 24.16.0 and npm 11.13.0 were installed but not used for this test-only change.

## Evidence for Task T1-03 (26 September 2026)

- **Target and baseline.** Worktree `work/fix/project/task-reassignment` began clean at `3061833`. Before adding coverage, the focused current-code tests passed: `TaskCreationIntegrationTest` 46/46, `ProjectInvitationExitIntegrationTest` 37/37, and `TaskWorkLogIntegrationTest` 23/23. The final focused runs passed 49/49, 37/37, and 23/23 respectively. No production Java, schema, dependency, calendar module, or template changed; no `.env` was read and no database on port 55432 was used.
- **AC-TSK-001 / TSK-001 / DB-004.** Guard: the service rejects a Leader's Task creation with a membership from another Project. A separate JDBC insert bypasses application validation and is refused by V1's composite assignee/Project foreign key with PostgreSQL SQLState `23503`.
- **AC-TSK-015 / TSK-002.** Guard: one active Intern self-creates a Task in each of two Projects and another in the first; all three succeed with the correct Project-local membership as the single current assignee.
- **AC-TSK-004, reassignment half / TSK-009.** Guard: reassigning a `DONE` Task throws `TaskValidationException`; the complete Task DTO, retained `TaskHistoryView` and notification count remain unchanged. Reopening with a reason and its transition record are deferred to T1-04.
- **TSK-009 / TSK-019.** Guard: the current Leader can reassign to an eligible active member; a target with a pending exit and a closed membership are refused without changing the successful assignment or notifications. Existing authorization-matrix tests keep non-Leader refusals green.
- **Exit-batch Task side / TSK-009 / AC-TSK-011.** Guard: request-bound, versioned exit batches update the unfinished Task assignee. The worked `IN_PROGRESS` Task retains creator, status, comment, work log and human membership attribution; the Leader becomes the assignment actor with an assignment time, and approval leaves the resulting Task history identical. The `DONE` Task stays assigned to the departing membership.
- **AC-TSK-011.** Already covered in T1-02 by `TaskCreationIntegrationTest#creatorMayEditOnlyWhileStillCurrentAssigneeAndLeaderMayEditAnyUnfinishedTask`; it remains green in all focused runs.
- **RED and guards.** The baseline focused tests were green; the added cases were guards for existing behavior, with no production-behavior RED. An early run of the new multi-Project case failed with `TaskNotFoundException: Task or Project was not found` because its raw SQL fixture lacked a current Leader term and a joined date at the Testcontainers clock; the fixture was completed and all tests passed. No permission defect was found, so no finding was sent to part B's matrix ledger. No existing assertion was removed or weakened.
- **Runs and tools.** The final focused command `./mvnw -B -Dtest=TaskCreationIntegrationTest,ProjectInvitationExitIntegrationTest,TaskWorkLogIntegrationTest test` passed 109 tests with 0 failures, 0 errors and 0 skips. Full `./mvnw -B test` passed 855 tests with 0 failures, 0 errors and 0 skips; `git diff --check` passed. Maven wrapper resolved Maven 3.9.16 on Temurin Java 25.0.4.1; Testcontainers 2.0.5 used PostgreSQL 18.4 with Docker server 29.7.2.
